Numbers cross-references

Numbers 1:1–10:36 · 419 completed verses · 727 completed pairs

Initial curated pass. Next: Numbers 11:1. Later source verses remain pending. Greek-based Saints-Translation numbering is retained.

Completed means the initial curated cross-reference pass: the source verse and every listed target passage were read in the pinned Saints-Translation English records, and each retained pair has a specific explanation. It does not mean every possible biblical connection has been exhausted, or independent scholarly approval.

Numbers 1:1

And the Lord spoke unto Moses in the wilderness of Sinai, in the Tent of Testimony, on the first day of the second month of the second year after they came out of the land of Egypt, saying:

  1. Exodus 40:17 Earlier tent chronology

    The tent was erected in the first month of the second year; this census revelation occurs there in that year’s second month.

    Read matching passage

    Exodus 40:17 In the first month of the second year after they came out of Egypt, at the new moon, the tent was erected.

    Pinned source record

  2. Numbers 10:11-12 Later Sinai departure

    The census begins on the second month’s first day at Sinai; the later departure begins there on that month’s twentieth day.

    Read matching passage

    Numbers 10:11 And in the second year, in the second month, on the twentieth day of the month, the cloud rose from the Tent of Testimony.

    Numbers 10:12 And the sons of Israel set out with their equipment in the wilderness of Sinai, and the cloud stood in the wilderness of Paran.

    Pinned source record

Numbers 1:2

Take the sum of the whole congregation of the sons of Israel, by their families, by their ancestral houses, according to the number of their names, head by head, every male

  1. Numbers 26:2 Later census mandate

    Both mandates count Israel by ancestral houses; the later command explicitly repeats the twenty-year military criterion continued in the next source verse.

    Read matching passage

    Numbers 26:2 Take the sum of the whole congregation of the sons of Israel, from twenty years old and upward, by their ancestral houses, everyone who goes out to line up for battle in Israel.

    Pinned source record

Numbers 1:3

from twenty years old and upward, every one who goes out in Israel’s army: register them with their army; you and Aaron shall register them.

  1. Numbers 26:2 Repeated military census criterion

    Both census commands count Israelites from twenty years upward who go out for battle, although the later census involves Eleazar rather than Aaron.

    Read matching passage

    Numbers 26:2 Take the sum of the whole congregation of the sons of Israel, from twenty years old and upward, by their ancestral houses, everyone who goes out to line up for battle in Israel.

    Pinned source record

  2. Exodus 30:14 Earlier census age criterion

    The earlier census contribution applies from twenty years upward, the same lower age boundary used here for military registration.

    Read matching passage

    Exodus 30:14 Everyone counted, from twenty years old and upward, shall give the contribution to the Lord.

    Pinned source record

Numbers 1:4

And one of the rulers from each tribe shall be with you; they shall be according to their ancestral houses.

  1. Numbers 7:2 Census rulers identified later

    The dedication account identifies its twelve ancestral-house rulers as the tribal rulers who stood over the enrolment, the office prescribed here.

    Read matching passage

    Numbers 7:2 and the rulers of Israel presented offerings, twelve rulers of their ancestral houses; these are the rulers of the tribes, these are they who stood over the enrolment.

    Pinned source record

Numbers 1:5

And these are the names of the men who shall stand with you. For Reuben: Elizur son of Shedeur.

  1. Numbers 2:10 Census leader and camp ruler

    Elizur son of Shedeur, selected here for Reuben’s census, is named again as that same tribe’s ruler in its camp arrangement.

    Read matching passage

    Numbers 2:10 The formation of the camp of Reuben, with their army, shall be toward the southwest. The ruler of the sons of Reuben is Elizur son of Shedeur.

    Pinned source record

  2. Numbers 7:30 Census leader and dedication donor

    The dedication list names Elizur son of Shedeur as Reuben’s ruler, identifying the same named tribal representative appointed here.

    Read matching passage

    Numbers 7:30 On the fourth day: Elizur son of Shedeur, ruler of the sons of Reuben.

    Pinned source record

  3. Numbers 10:18 Census leader and march commander

    Reuben’s army later marches under Elizur son of Shedeur, the same man named here to assist with that tribe’s registration.

    Read matching passage

    Numbers 10:18 And the formation of Reuben’s camp set out, with their army. Elizur son of Shedeur was over their army.

    Pinned source record

Numbers 1:18

And they assembled the whole congregation on the first day of the month of the second year and registered them by their descent and ancestral houses, counting their names, from twenty years old and upward, every male, head by head.

  1. Numbers 1:2-3 Same-chapter census command fulfilled

    The gathering and registration by descent, ancestral houses, individual names, and age twenty upward implements the census procedure commanded earlier in this chapter.

    Read matching passage

    Numbers 1:2 Take the sum of the whole congregation of the sons of Israel, by their families, by their ancestral houses, according to the number of their names, head by head, every male

    Numbers 1:3 from twenty years old and upward, every one who goes out in Israel’s army: register them with their army; you and Aaron shall register them.

    Pinned source record

Numbers 1:19

As the Lord instructed Moses, they were registered in the wilderness of Sinai.

  1. Numbers 1:2-3 Same-chapter census instruction fulfilled

    This statement of registration at the Lord’s instruction concludes the execution of the specifically prescribed ancestral-house and military-age census.

    Read matching passage

    Numbers 1:2 Take the sum of the whole congregation of the sons of Israel, by their families, by their ancestral houses, according to the number of their names, head by head, every male

    Numbers 1:3 from twenty years old and upward, every one who goes out in Israel’s army: register them with their army; you and Aaron shall register them.

    Pinned source record

Numbers 1:20

And the sons of Reuben, Israel’s firstborn, were reckoned by their families, by their divisions, by their ancestral houses, by the number of their names, head by head, every male from twenty years old and upward, every one who goes out in the army:

  1. Numbers 26:5-7 Later census of the same tribe

    The later census records Reuben’s named clans and their registration, continuing the tribal and ancestral-family organization specified for this earlier military census.

    Read matching passage

    Numbers 26:5 Reuben was Israel's firstborn. The sons of Reuben: Hanoch and the clan of Hanoch; from Pallu, the clan of Pallu;

    Numbers 26:6 from Hezron, the clan of Hezron; from Carmi, the clan of Carmi.

    Numbers 26:7 These were Reuben's clans. Their registration numbered forty-three thousand seven hundred thirty.

    Pinned source record

  2. Exodus 6:14 Reuben’s firstborn and family identity

    Both passages identify Reuben as Israel’s firstborn and organize his descendants by ancestral family, with the earlier genealogy naming his four sons.

    Read matching passage

    Exodus 6:14 These are the heads of their ancestral houses. The sons of Reuben, Israel’s firstborn: Hanoch, Pallu, Hezron, and Carmi. This is the kindred of Reuben.

    Pinned source record

Numbers 1:21

The enrolment from the tribe of Reuben was forty-six thousand five hundred.

  1. Numbers 2:11 Identical census figure in camp arrangement

    Reuben’s military enrolment is forty-six thousand five hundred here; the camp arrangement assigns precisely that same registered number to Reuben’s army.

    Read matching passage

    Numbers 2:11 His army, those registered, numbered forty-six thousand five hundred.

    Pinned source record

  2. Numbers 26:7 Contrasting later census figure

    The earlier Reuben registration is forty-six thousand five hundred; the later census gives forty-three thousand seven hundred thirty for the same tribe, recording a different enrolment.

    Read matching passage

    Numbers 26:7 These were Reuben's clans. Their registration numbered forty-three thousand seven hundred thirty.

    Pinned source record

    Read matching passage

    Numbers 2:19 His army, those registered, numbered forty thousand five hundred.

    Read matching passage

    Numbers 26:41 These were Ephraim's clans according to their registration: thirty-two thousand five hundred. These were the clans of the sons of Joseph by their clans.

And the Lord spoke unto Moses, saying:

  1. Numbers 1:49-53 Same-chapter speech introduction and particular charge

    This introduction announces the Lord’s specific instruction to exclude Levi from the military census and assign the tribe responsibility around the Tent of Testimony.

    Read matching passage

    Numbers 1:49 See that you do not register the tribe of Levi together with them, neither shall you take their number among the sons of Israel.

    Numbers 1:50 And you shall appoint the Levites over the Tent of Testimony, and over all its furnishings, and over all that is therein. They shall carry the Tent and all its furnishings, and they shall minister therein, and shall camp round about the Tent.

    Numbers 1:51 And when the Tent sets out, the Levites shall take it down; when it camps, they shall erect it. Let an outsider who approaches die.

    Numbers 1:52 And the sons of Israel shall camp, each in his own formation and each according to his own command, with their army.

    Numbers 1:53 But let the Levites camp before the Lord round about the Tent of Testimony, and there shall be no offence among the sons of Israel. And the Levites themselves shall keep the charge of the Tent of Testimony.

Numbers 1:51

And when the Tent sets out, the Levites shall take it down; when it camps, they shall erect it. Let an outsider who approaches die.

  1. Numbers 10:17-21 Tent dismantling and transport in departure

    The departure account assigns tent transport to Gershon and Merari and holy-object transport to Kohath, describing the movement entrusted generally to Levites here.

    Read matching passage

    Numbers 10:17 And they shall take down the Tent, and the sons of Gershon and Merari shall set out carrying the Tent.

    Numbers 10:18 And the formation of Reuben’s camp set out, with their army. Elizur son of Shedeur was over their army.

    Numbers 10:19 And Shelumiel son of Zurishaddai was over the army of the tribe of the sons of Simeon.

    Numbers 10:20 And Eliasaph son of Reuel was over the army of the tribe of the sons of Gad.

    Numbers 10:21 And the sons of Kohath shall set out carrying the holy things; they shall erect the Tent before they arrive.

    Pinned source record

  2. Numbers 4:15 Holy objects and lethal approach boundary

    Kohath may carry the covered holy things but must not touch them lest they die, specifying a transport boundary alongside this outsider prohibition.

    Read matching passage

    Numbers 4:15 And when the camp sets out, Aaron and his sons shall finish covering the holy things and all the holy utensils. Afterward the sons of Kohath shall enter to carry them, and they shall not touch the holy things, so that they do not die. These are what the sons of Kohath shall carry in the Tent of Testimony.

Numbers 1:53

But let the Levites camp before the Lord round about the Tent of Testimony, and there shall be no offence among the sons of Israel. And the Levites themselves shall keep the charge of the Tent of Testimony.

  1. Numbers 18:3-5 Guarding the sanctuary prevents wrath

    The later charge assigns Levites tent custody, restricts access to holy vessels and altar, and connects guarding holy things with preventing wrath in Israel.

    Read matching passage

    Numbers 18:3 And they shall keep your charges and the charges of the Tent; only they shall not approach the holy vessels and the altar, that neither they nor you may die.

    Numbers 18:4 And they shall join you, and shall keep the charges of the Tent of Testimony according to all the ministries of the Tent; and the outsider shall not approach you.

    Numbers 18:5 And you shall keep the charges of the holy things and the charges of the altar, and there shall be no wrath among the sons of Israel.

    Pinned source record

  2. Numbers 3:38 Sanctuary guard and lethal outsider boundary

    Moses, Aaron, and his sons keep the sanctuary’s charge on the tent’s east, while outsiders face death; this supplies a particular part of the protective arrangement.

    Read matching passage

    Numbers 3:38 And those camping before the Tent of Testimony on the east are Moses and Aaron and his sons, keeping the charge of the sanctuary for the charges of the sons of Israel. And the outsider who touches shall die.

    Pinned source record

    Leviticus narrates Nadab and Abihu offering uncommanded strange fire and dying, then addresses Eleazar and Ithamar as Aaron’s remaining sons under priestly restrictions.

    Read matching passage

    Leviticus 10:1 And the two sons of Aaron, Nadab and Abihu, each took his firepan, and put fire upon it, and placed incense upon it, and presented strange fire before the Lord, which the Lord had not commanded them.

    Leviticus 10:2 And fire came out from the Lord and consumed them, and they died before the Lord.

    Leviticus 10:3 And Moses said unto Aaron: This is what the Lord said, saying: Among those who draw near unto Me I will be sanctified, and in the whole congregation I will be glorified. And Aaron was deeply stricken.

    Leviticus 10:4 And Moses called Misadai and Elzaphan, sons of Uzziel, sons of the brother of Aaron’s father, and said unto them: Draw near, and carry your brethren away from before the holy things, outside the camp.

    Leviticus 10:5 And they came near and carried them in their tunics outside the camp, as Moses had said.

    Leviticus 10:6 And Moses said unto Aaron, and unto Eleazar and Ithamar his remaining sons: You shall not remove your turbans, neither shall you rend your garments, lest you die and wrath come upon the whole congregation. Your brethren, the whole house of Israel, shall weep over the burning by which they were burned by the Lord.

    Leviticus 10:7 And you shall not go out from the door of the Tent of Testimony, lest you die; for the anointing oil from the Lord is upon you. And they did according to the word of Moses.

    Numbers 18:7 And you and your sons with you shall keep your priesthood in every matter of the altar and within the veil; and you shall perform the ministries, a gift of your priesthood; and the outsider who approaches shall die.

    Pinned source record

  2. Numbers 4:15 Holy-object transport and lethal touch boundary

    Aaron and his sons must cover the holy objects before Kohath carries them; touching them brings death, supplying a particular transport application of the sanctuary boundary.

    Read matching passage

Afterward the sons of Kohath shall enter to carry them, and they shall not touch the holy things, so that they do not die. These are what the sons of Kohath shall carry in the Tent of Testimony.

Numbers 5:2

Command the sons of Israel to send out of the camp everyone with leprosy, everyone with a seminal discharge, and everyone unclean through a dead person.

  1. Leviticus 13:45-46 parallel-camp-exclusion

    Leviticus specifically requires the person with leprosy to dwell outside the camp, matching one class excluded here.

    Read matching passage

    Leviticus 13:45 And he that has leprosy, in whom the lesion is, shall have his garments loosened and his head uncovered, and shall put a covering about his mouth, and shall be called unclean;

    Leviticus 13:46 all the days in which the lesion is upon him, being unclean, he shall be unclean; he shall dwell apart; his dwelling shall be outside the camp.

    Pinned source record

  2. Leviticus 15:2-4 identified-uncleanness

    The flow and seminal-discharge uncleanness defined in Leviticus supplies another category expressly sent outside the camp here.

    Read matching passage

    Leviticus 15:2 Speak unto the sons of Israel, and you shall say unto them: If any man has a flow from his body, his flow is unclean.

    Leviticus 15:3 And this is the law of his uncleanness: discharging seed from his body through the flow by which his body has become stopped up, this is his uncleanness in him; all the days of the flow of his body, in which his body has become stopped up through the flow, are his uncleanness.

    Leviticus 15:4 Every bed on which the man with a seminal discharge lies is unclean, and every article on which the man with a seminal discharge sits shall be unclean.

    Pinned source record

  3. Numbers 19:11-13 corpse-uncleanness

    Numbers later defines corpse-contact uncleanness and warns that failure to purify defiles the Tent of the Lord.

    Read matching passage

    Numbers 19:11 Whosoever touches a dead person, any human being who has died, shall be unclean for seven days.

    Numbers 19:12 That person shall be purified on the third day and on the seventh day, and he shall be clean. If he is not purified on the third day and on the seventh day, he shall not be clean.

    Numbers 19:13 Whosoever touches the dead body of any human being who has died, and is not purified, has defiled the Tent of the Lord; that soul shall be wiped out from Israel. Because water of sprinkling was not sprinkled upon him, he is unclean; his uncleanness is still in him.

    Pinned source record

Numbers 5:3

From male unto female, send them outside the camp, and they shall in no wise defile their camps, wherein I dwell among them.

  1. Deuteronomy 23:11-15 divine-presence-and-camp-holiness

    Both passages require exclusion associated with uncleanness because the Lord is present within Israel's camp.

    Read matching passage

    Deuteronomy 23:11 If there is among you a man who is not clean by reason of a discharge in the night, he shall go forth outside the camp, and shall not enter the camp.

    Deuteronomy 23:12 And it shall be, toward evening, that he shall wash his body with water; and when the sun is set, he shall enter the camp.

    Deuteronomy 23:13 And you shall have a place outside the camp, whither you shall go forth.

    Deuteronomy 23:14 And you shall have a peg upon your belt; and when you squat outside, you shall dig therewith, and turn and cover your excrement with it.

    Deuteronomy 23:15 For the Lord your God walks in the midst of your camp, to deliver you, and to give your enemy before you; therefore your camp shall be holy, that no indecent thing be seen among you, and He turn away from you.

    Pinned source record

Numbers 5:5

And the Lord spoke unto Moses, saying:

  1. Numbers 5:6-8 Same-chapter introduction-to-specific-instruction

    This speech introduces guilt, confession, restitution with an added fifth, and the case lacking a recipient's next of kin.

    Read matching passage

    Numbers 5:6 Speak unto the sons of Israel, saying: If a man or woman commits any of the sins of men, and acts with utter disregard, and that soul incurs guilt,

    Numbers 5:7 that person shall confess the sin committed, and repay the principal of the offence, and add a fifth part thereto, and give it unto the one who was wronged.

    Numbers 5:8 But if the man has no next of kin to receive repayment for the offence, the repayment made unto the Lord shall be the priest’s, besides the ram of atonement, wherewith he shall make atonement for him.

    Pinned source record

  1. Leviticus 5:21-24 parallel-guilt-and-restitution

    Leviticus specifies fraudulent wrongs against a neighbour and requires repayment with an added fifth when guilt is incurred.

    Read matching passage

    Leviticus 5:21 If a soul sins, and utterly disregards the commandments of the Lord, and deals falsely with his neighbour concerning a deposit, or a partnership, or robbery, or any wrong which he has done unto his neighbour,

    Leviticus 5:22 or finds a lost thing and deals falsely concerning it, and swears unjustly concerning any of all the things which a man may do so as to sin therein,

    Leviticus 5:23 then it shall be, when he has sinned and incurred guilt, that he shall return the plunder which he seized, or that which he obtained by wrong, or the deposit which was entrusted unto him, or the lost thing which he found,

    Leviticus 5:24 concerning every matter about which he swore unjustly. And he shall repay the principal, and add a fifth part unto it; he shall repay it unto the one to whom it belongs, on the day when he is convicted.

    Pinned source record

    Leviticus 5:26 And the priest shall make atonement for him before the Lord, and he shall be forgiven concerning any of all the things which he has done and wherein he has offended Him.

  1. Judges 13:5-7 Consecration parallel

    The voluntary consecration vow here illuminates Samson's designation as a Nazirite of God, while his lifelong calling differs from a limited vow.

    Read matching passage

    Judges 13:5 For behold, you shall conceive and bear a son, and no iron shall come upon his head; for the child shall be a sanctified Nazirite to God from the womb, and he shall begin to save Israel from the hand of the foreigners.”

    Judges 13:6 The woman came and told her husband, saying, “A man of God came to me. His appearance was like the appearance of God’s messenger, very glorious. I asked where he was from, and he did not tell me his name.

    Judges 13:7 And he said to me, ‘Behold, you shall conceive and bear a son. Now drink no wine or sikera, and eat nothing unclean; for the child shall be a Nazirite of God from the womb until the day of his death.’”

    The consecration vow of a man or woman supplies the legal setting for the four vowed men whose purification and offerings Paul supports.

    Read matching passage

    Acts 21:23 Therefore do what we tell you. We have four men who have taken a vow upon themselves.

    Acts 21:24 Take these men, purify yourself with them, and pay their expenses so that they may shave their heads. Then everyone will know that there is nothing to what they have been told about you, but that you yourself also walk in step, keeping the law.

    Acts 21:25 But concerning the Gentiles who have believed, we have sent word, judging that they should keep themselves from what is sacrificed to idols, blood, what is strangled, and sexual immorality.”

    Acts 21:26 Then Paul took the men, and on the next day, after purifying himself with them, he entered the temple, giving notice of the completion of the days of purification, up to the presentation of the offering for each one of them.

Numbers 7:5

Take them from them, and they shall be for the works of ministry of the Tent of Testimony; and you shall give them unto the Levites, unto each according to his ministry.

  1. Numbers 4:24-28 Service provision

    Assigning transport according to ministry corresponds to Gershon's specified carrying duties for curtains, coverings, hangings, and related utensils.

    Read matching passage

    Numbers 4:24 This is the ministry of the division of Gershon, in ministering and carrying:

    Numbers 4:25 And it shall carry the skin curtains of the Tent, the Tent of Testimony, its covering, the blue covering above it, and the covering at the door of the Tent of Testimony,

    Numbers 4:26 And the court hangings by the Tent of Testimony, the remaining things, and all the ministering utensils used with them. They shall do the work.

    Numbers 4:27 The ministry of the sons of Gershon shall be at the mouth of Aaron and his sons, in all their ministries and all that they carry. And you shall register them by name, with all that they carry.

    Numbers 4:28 This is the ministry of the sons of Gershon in the Tent of Testimony. Their charge is under the hand of Ithamar son of Aaron the priest.

    Pinned source record

  2. Numbers 4:29-33 Service provision

    Giving transport according to ministry also corresponds to Merari's distinct responsibility for the Tent's structural supports, pillars, bases, pegs, and cords.

    Read matching passage

    Numbers 4:29 You shall register the sons of Merari by their divisions, by their ancestral houses.

    Numbers 4:30 You shall register them from twenty-five years old and upward unto fifty years old, every one who enters to minister in the work of the Tent of Testimony.

    Numbers 4:31 And these are the things entrusted to their carrying for all their work in the Tent of Testimony: the capitals of the Tent, its bars, pillars, and bases, the covering, their bases and pillars, and the covering at the door of the Tent,

    Numbers 4:32 And the pillars around the court and their bases, the pillars of the curtain at the court gate and their bases, their pegs and cords, all their utensils, and all their ministries. Register them by name, with all the utensils entrusted to their carrying.

    Numbers 4:33 This is the ministry of the division of the sons of Merari, all their work in the Tent of Testimony, under the hand of Ithamar son of Aaron the priest.

Numbers 7:9

And unto the sons of Kohath he gave none, for they have the ministries of the sanctuary; they shall bear them upon their shoulders.

  1. Numbers 4:4-15 Transport restriction explained

    Kohath receives no wagons because its holy objects must be carried; the earlier instructions prepare those objects with carrying poles for Kohath's ministry.

    Read matching passage

    Numbers 4:4 And this is the work of the sons of Kohath in the Tent of Testimony: the most holy things.

    Numbers 4:5 And when the camp sets out, Aaron and his sons shall enter, and take down the overshadowing veil, and cover the ark of testimony with it.

    Numbers 4:6 And they shall put over it a covering of blue skin, lay a cloth entirely of blue above it, and insert the carrying poles.

    Numbers 4:7 And over the table set forth they shall spread a cloth entirely of purple, and the dishes, and the incense vessels, and the cups, and the vessels wherein he pours the drink offerings; and the continual loaves shall be upon it.

    Numbers 4:8 And they shall spread a scarlet cloth over it, cover it with a covering of blue leather, and insert its carrying poles through it.

    Numbers 4:9 And they shall take a blue cloth and cover the illuminating lampstand, its lamps, tongs, snuffers, and all the oil vessels used in ministering with them.

    Numbers 4:10 And they shall put it and all its furnishings into a covering of blue leather and place it on carrying poles.

    Numbers 4:11 And over the golden altar they shall spread a blue cloth, cover it with a covering of blue leather, and insert its carrying poles.

    Numbers 4:12 And they shall take all the ministering utensils used in the holy place, put them in a blue cloth, cover them with a covering of blue leather, and place them on carrying poles.

    Numbers 4:13 And he shall put the cover upon the altar, and they shall spread a cloth entirely of purple over it.

    Numbers 4:14 And they shall put on it all the utensils used in its ministry: the firepans, meat forks, bowls, the cover, and all the altar utensils. They shall spread a covering of blue leather over it and insert its carrying poles. They shall take a purple cloth, cover the basin and its stand together, put them into a covering of blue leather, and place them on carrying poles.

    Numbers 4:15 And when the camp sets out, Aaron and his sons shall finish covering the holy things and all the holy utensils. Afterward the sons of Kohath shall enter to carry them, and they shall not touch the holy things, so that they do not die. These are what the sons of Kohath shall carry in the Tent of Testimony.

    Pinned source record

    Read matching passage

    1 Kings 8:64 That day the king consecrated the middle of the court in front of the Lord’s house, for there he offered the whole burnt offering, sacrifices, and fat of the peace offerings; the bronze altar before the Lord was too small to bear the whole burnt offering and the sacrifices of peace offerings.

    Leviticus 3:3 And from the sacrifice of deliverance they shall bring an offering unto the Lord: the fat which covers the belly, and all the fat upon the belly,

    Leviticus 3:4 and the two kidneys, and the fat upon them which is by the thighs, and the lobe upon the liver, which he shall remove with the kidneys.

    Leviticus 3:6 And if his gift is from the flock, as a sacrifice of deliverance unto the Lord, whether male or female, he shall bring it unblemished.

Numbers 7:21

one calf, one ram, and one year-old male lamb for a whole burnt offering;

  1. Numbers 7:87 Same-chapter individual and aggregate

    On the second day, Nethanel son of Zuar presents whole burnt animals. The calf, ram, and year-old male lamb for whole burning each contribute one to the closing totals of twelve animals of each kind.

    Read matching passage

    Numbers 7:87 All the cattle for whole burning were twelve calves, with twelve rams and twelve year-old male lambs, and their sacrifices and drink offerings; also twelve male goats from the goats for sin.

    Pinned source record

  2. Numbers 7:15 Same-chapter repeated dedication gift

    Nethanel son of Zuar's second-day gift matches the first-day presentation by Nahshon. This donor's whole burnt offering repeats Nahshon's exact three animals: one calf, one ram, and one year-old male lamb.

    Read matching passage

    Numbers 7:15 one calf, one ram, and one year-old male lamb for a whole burnt offering;

    Pinned source record

Numbers 7:22

and one male goat from the goats for sin;

  1. Numbers 7:87 Same-chapter individual and aggregate

    On the second day, Nethanel son of Zuar presents a sin offering goat. The single male goat for sin is one of the twelve male goats counted for sin in the dedication's final aggregate.

    Read matching passage

    Numbers 7:87 All the cattle for whole burning were twelve calves, with twelve rams and twelve year-old male lambs, and their sacrifices and drink offerings; also twelve male goats from the goats for sin.

    Pinned source record

  2. Numbers 7:16 Same-chapter repeated dedication gift

    Nethanel son of Zuar's second-day gift matches the first-day presentation by Nahshon. This donor's single male goat for sin repeats the precise animal, sex, number, and purpose of Nahshon's first-day sin offering.

    Read matching passage

    Numbers 7:16 and one male goat from the goats for sin;

    Pinned source record

Numbers 7:23

and for a sacrifice of deliverance: two heifers, five rams, five male goats, and five year-old ewe lambs. This was the gift of Nethanel son of Zuar.

  1. Numbers 7:88 Same-chapter individual and aggregate

    On the second day, Nethanel son of Zuar presents deliverance animals. The two heifers and five each of rams, male goats, and year-old ewe lambs contribute to the final totals of 24 and 60 each.

    Read matching passage

    Numbers 7:88 All the cattle for the sacrifice of deliverance were twenty-four heifers, with sixty rams, sixty male goats, and sixty unblemished year-old ewe lambs. This was the dedication of the altar after the filling of his hands and after anointing him.

    Pinned source record

  2. Numbers 7:17 Same-chapter repeated dedication gift

    Nethanel son of Zuar's second-day gift matches the first-day presentation by Nahshon. This deliverance offering repeats the first donor's two heifers and five each of rams, male goats, and year-old ewe lambs.

    Read matching passage

    Numbers 7:17 and for a sacrifice of deliverance: two heifers, five rams, five male goats, and five year-old ewe lambs. This was the gift of Nahshon son of Amminadab.

Numbers 7:25

His gift was one silver dish weighing one hundred thirty shekels and one silver bowl of seventy sanctuary shekels, both full of fine flour prepared with oil for a sacrifice;

    Read matching passage
